makes for easy identification and understanding, thereby helping to forestall any possible
ambiguity or confusion.

P
     reparatory activities for the October 9, 2011 presidential election run from the 2nd-half
     of August 2010 to the convening of the electorate, on 30 August 2011. These activities,
     considered below, mostly involved the revision of voters’ registers, computerisation of
the upgraded electoral roll and the preparation of election logistics.

         ll things being equal, the Directorate                       It is essential, as a result, to consolidate
         General of Elections has lived up to                      this spirit of patriotism through legal, institu-
         the challenge of organising, mana-                        tional or other forms of adjustments and to
ging and supervising all the electoral ope-                        refine the management of the electoral
rations of the October 9, 2011 presidential                        process in Cameroon. The corrective inno-
election on time despite certain structural                        vations introduced by ELECAM, ever since
and contextual shortcomings observed and                           taking over election management, can
its youthfulness as an election management                         only be used as a springboard.
institution.
                                                                       An improvement of the Cameroonian
   Nonetheless, the credit for this achieve-                       electoral process cannot do without the
ment goes, first and foremost, to the Came-                        conscious and constructive participation of
roonian electorate, for heeding the call of                        all its stakeholders, notably that of political
ELECAM to take an active part in the elec-                         parties whose involvement is currently ne-
tion, at a time when calls for boycott were                        glected, if its objective has to be met.
resounding and when the reputation of the
election management institution was put to                            It would thus be necessary to encourage
question.                                                          these parties to fully play their respective
                                                                   roles in view of the attainment of the demo-
   More so, this electorate showed proof of                        cratic ideal.

led to triumph over its civic responsibility.

- COPY OF DECrEE NO. 2011/277 OF 30 AUGUST 2011
  TO CONvENE THE ELECTOrATE FOr THE ELECTiON OF

 AUGUST 2011
                                                                                                   ANNEXE IV




REPUBLIC OF CAMEROON                                                              PEACE – WORK – FATHERLAND
      ----------                                                                          ----------

                                                        DECREE No. 2011/277 of 30 AUGUST 2011
                                                        to convene the electorate for the election of the
                                                        President of the Republic


                                        THE PRESIDENT OF THE REPUBLIC,

Mindful          of the Constitution;

Mindful          of Law No. 92/010 of 17 September 1992 to lay down conditions governing
                 the vacancy of and election to the Presidency of the Republic, amended
                 and supplemented by Law No. 97/020 of 9 September 1997 and by Law No.
                 2011/002 of 6 May 2011;

Mindful          of Law No. 2006/011 of 29 December 2006 to set up and lay down to organi-
                 zation and functioning of Elections Cameroon (ELECAM), amended and sup-
                 plemented by Law No. 2008/005 of 29 June 2008, by Law No. 2010/005 of 13
                 April 2010 and by Law No. 2011/013 of 6 May 2011;

Mindful          of Law No. 2011/013 of 13 July 2011 relating to voting by Cameroonians citi-
                 zens settled or residing abroad,

                                         HEREBY DECREES AS FOLLOWS:

ARTICLE 1.- Electors are hereby convened, on Sunday, 9 October 2011, to elect the Presi-
            dent of the Republic.

ARTICLE 2.- Polling stations shall be opened at 8 a.m. and closed at 6 p.m.

ARTICLE 3.- This decree shall be registered, published according to the procedure of ur-
            gency and inserted in the official Gazette in English and French./-

                                                                                            Yaounde, 30 August 2011

                                                                                                                       (ed)

                                                                                                                  PAUL BIYA,

                                                                                   THE PRESIDENT OF THE REPUBLIC

                      governing the vacancy of and election to the Presidency of the

                      down the organization and functioning of Elections Cameroon

                      Cameroonians settled or residing abroad;

    Mindful           of Decrees No. 2008/463 of 30 December 2008 and No. 2011/204
                      of July 2011 appointing Members of the Electoral Board of
                      Elections Cameroon (ELECAM);

                      President and Vice-President of the Electoral Board of Elections
                      Cameroon (ELECAM);

    Mindful           of Decree No. 2008/470 of 31 December 2008 appointing officials
                      at Elections Cameroon (ELECAM);

                      electorate for the election of the President of the Republic,

    shall be eligible to the October 9, 2011 presidential election:

Direction Générale des Elections                                                   Directorate General of Elections
               -------                                                                            ------



                                                    DECISION N° 0418/ELECAM/DGE of 15 SEPT. 2011
                                                     to lay down the organisation of polling stations
                                                         within the national territory on the occasion
                                                           of the October 2011 presidential election


                                 THE DIRECTOR GENERAL OF ELECTIONS,



Mindful of       the Constitution;
Mindful of       Law No. 92/010 of 17 September 1992 to lay down conditions gover-
                 ning the vacancy of and election to the Presidency of the Republic
                 and its subsequent amendments;
Mindful of       Law No. 2006/11 of 29 December 2006 to set up and lay down the or-
                 ganisation and functioning of Elections Cameroon (ELECAM) and its
                 subsequent amendments;
Mindful of       Decree No. 2008/470 of 31 December 2006 appointing Officials at
                 Elections Cameroon;
Mindful of       Decree No. 2011/277 of 30 August convening electors to the election
                 of the President of the Republic,


                                       HEREBY DECIDES AS FOLLOWS:


                                                       CHAPTER I

                                             GENERAL PROVISIONS


ARTICLE 1.- (1) This decision determines the organisation of polling stations, on the
                   occasion of the October 2011 presidential election.
(2) It is hereby taken, in accordance with Section 22 (1) of the abovementioned
    Law No. 2006/011 of 29 December 2006, to define, notably:
          - activities preceding polling operations;
          - polling procedure;
          - vote counting procedure.



  GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS   153
           ANNEX XII




                                                  CHAPTER II

                        ACTIVITIES PRECEDING POLLING OPERATIONS


                                                  SECTION I

                                        ELECTION MATERIALS

ARTICLE 2.- (1) Each polling station shall be furnished with the following materials:
       - one ballot box;
       - one polling booth;
       - seals;
       - one disposal bag;
       - one stamp pad with indelible ink;
       - one date stamp;
       - ball pens;
       - one calculator;
       - one lamp, if necessary;
       - two (2) copies of the electoral roll;
       - one copy of the decision to lay down the organisation of polling stations;
       - one copy of the Guide to Chairpersons and Members of Local Polling
         Commissions;
       - ballot papers of each running candidate;
       - vote count return reports;
       - vote counting sheets;
       - appropriate envelopes;
       - office stationery.

(2) The materials listed in Subparagraph (1) above shall be placed on the table
    where members of the Local Polling Commission are sitting, with the exception
    of the polling booth, the disposal bag and the ballot box.

(3) The ballot box, envelopes and ballot papers shall be kept under the permanent
    supervision of the Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission.

(4) The ballot box shall be placed in the middle of the hall, in front of the table where
    the polling commission members are seated.

ARTICLE 3.- (1) The polling booth shall be placed in the hall wherein the polling takes
                place and in full view of the electors. It shall be placed such as to
                shield the gestures of the elector from the view of the other electors,
                members of the Local Polling Commission and the general public.
(2) One disposal bag shall be placed within each polling booth into which each
    elector must throw the unused ballot papers.



  154      GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS
                                                                                                   ANNEX XII




                                                     SECTION II

                                          OPENING OF THE POLL

ARTICLE 4.- The poll must be open at 8:00 a.m. prompt.


ARTICLE 5.- (1) Before the first vote is cast, the Chairperson of the Local Polling Com-
                mission shall open the ballot box to let all the members of the Com-
                mission, duly accredited national and international observers and the
                electors present ascertain that it is empty and is provided with a single
                opening for the envelopes.
(2) The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall then close the ballot box
    again under the watchful eyes of the persons mentioned in Subparagraph (1)
    above and seal it.


                                                      CHAPTER III

                                              POLLING PROCEDURE


                                                      SECTION I

                          ACCOMPLISHING THE VOTING EXERCISE


ARTICLE 6.- (1) Before casting his/her vote, the elector shall be identified by the
                Chairperson or a member of the Local Polling Commission who should
                ensure that his/her full names and voter’s card number are consistent
                with those entered in the electoral roll.
(2) Once the formality provided for in Subparagraph (1) is completed, the elector
    shall, under the supervision of the Chairperson and members of the Local Polling
    Commission, personally take the ballot paper of each candidate and an enve-
    lope.
(3) With the election materials specified in Subparagraph (2) above, the elector shall
    enter the polling booth and select the ballot paper of his/her choice.
(4) He/she shall then come out of the polling booth and slip the envelope containing
    the chosen ballot paper into the ballot box.

ARTICLE 7.- The vote of the elector shall be evidenced by:
       - affixing the sign (X) before his/her name on the electoral roll, in the column
         provided for that purpose;
       - entering the date of the poll on the voter’s card in the space provided for
         that purpose;


  GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS   155
            ANNEX XII




        - affixing the elector’s thumb print, taken from the upper part of his/her thumb
          where the nail ends and marked with an indelible ink, on the voter’s card;
        - marking the elector’s thumbnail with indelible ink.

ARTICLE 8.- Any elector duly registered on the electoral roll, without a voter’s card,
            shall be allowed to vote, provided he/she is formally identified by the
            Commission.

ARTICLE 9.- (1) Any elector who is unable to personally accomplish the voting exer-
                cise specified in Article 6 above, may enlist the assistance of an elec-
                tor of his/her choice, registered in the electoral roll of the polling
                station concerned.
(2) The elector enlisted shall be neither a candidate nor the representative of a can-
    didate.
(3) He/she may not assist more than one person on the polling day.
(4) Voting by proxy shall not be allowed.

ARTICLE 10.- Besides, the chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall take all
             appropriate measures in favour of persons with disabilities, pregnant
             women and elderly voters.

ARTICLE 11.- After the vote is cast, the Local Polling Commission shall return the
             voter’s card to the elector; who shall immediately leave the hall. He/she
             shall not be allowed to talk to the Chairperson, members of the Local
             Polling Commission and observers.


                                                 SECTION III

   ROLE OF THE CHAIRPERSON OF THE LOCAL POLLING COMMISSION


ARTICLE 12.- (1) The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall ensure the pre-
                 sence of, at least, three members of the Local Polling Commission in
                 the station throughout the polling exercise.
(2) The Chairperson must strive to be present throughout the polling exercise.

ARTICLE 13.- (1) Only members of the Local Polling Commission shall be allowed to
                  remain in the hall where the polling exercise is taking place.
(2) Notwithstanding the provisions of Subparagraph (1) above, candidates or their
    representatives, competent ELECAM branch officials within the jurisdiction of the
    polling station, the administrative authority and observers may remain in the pol-
    ling station concerned, provided that such presence does not disrupt the
    conduct of the poll.




  156      GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS
                                                                                                   ANNEX XII




ARTICLE 14.- In case an individual carrying a weapon or dangerous device enters
             the polling station where voting is taking place, causes unlawful crowd
             gathering, threatens electors or attempts to forcefully take away the
             ballot box or any other election materials provided for in Article 2 (1)
             above, the Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall order the
             said individual to leave and, if necessary, in accordance with the laws
             in force, requisition the forces of law and order to restore order in the
             station or cause the station to be evacuated.

ARTICLE 15.- (1) At 6:00 p.m. prompt, the Chairperson of the Local Polling Commis-
                  sion shall declare the poll closed.
(2) However, electors already present inside the hall at the closing hour, as well as
    those waiting outside the door to enter the hall, shall be allowed to cast their
    votes.
(3) After declaring the poll closed, the Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission
    shall seal the opening of the ballot box and take all necessary measures for the
    smooth conduct of the vote counting exercise.
(4) Once the poll is closed, the hall in which the polling exercise took place shall re-
    main open until the vote counting is completed.


                                                      CHAPTER IV

                                        VOTE COUNTING PROCEDURE

ARTICLE 16.- Prior to the vote counting exercise, the Chairperson of the Local Polling
             Commission shall convene the appointed scrutineers and record their
             names in the report. He/she shall also prepare the vote counting sheets.

ARTICLE 17.- (1) At the end of the polling exercise, any elector who wishes to attend
                 the vote counting shall be allowed to enter the hall in which the
                 Local Polling Commission is conducting the vote count, depending
                 on the capacity of the said hall.
(2) The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission may, in case of disturbance,
    expel any troublemaker and/or requisition the intervention of the forces of law
    and order, in conformity with the law in force.

ARTICLE 18.- Before opening the ballot box for the vote counting exercise, the Chair-
             person of the Local Polling Commission concerned shall place a table,
             in the middle of the hall, on which the appointed scrutineers shall pour
             out all the ballot papers contained in the ballot box, for counting.

ARTICLE 19.- (1) The vote counting exercise shall be conducted in accordance with
                 the provisions of Sections 85 to 92 of Law No. 92/10 of 17 September
                 1992 to lay down conditions governing the vacancy of and election
                 to the Presidency of the Republic, and its subsequent amendments.


  GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS   157
           ANNEX XII




(2) The following shall not be taken into account:
         - ballot papers other than those printed officially;
         - ballot papers with any mark or sign;
         - ballot papers found in envelopes other than those put at the disposal of the
           electors;
         - ballot papers of the various candidates found in the same envelope.
(3) The ballot papers thus rejected and, where appropriate, the envelopes contai-
    ning them shall be signed by the members of the Local Polling Commission and
    attached to the report in which their number shall also be indicated.
(4) Ballot papers found in the ballot box without envelopes and empty envelopes
    shall be considered invalid and signed by the members of the competent Local
    Polling Commission. This shall be mentioned in the report.

ARTICLE 20.- (1) The results of the poll shall be immediately recorded in the report.
                  The said report shall be drawn up in as many copies as there are
                  members, plus three (3) other copies; signed by the Chairperson and
                  members of the Local Polling Commission.
(2) One copy of the report shall be handed to each of the members of the Local
    polling Commission present who signed it.
(3) The result of the poll thus obtained in the polling station shall be announced on
    the spot.

ARTICLE 21.- (1) At the end of the vote counting exercise, the Chairperson of the Local
                  Polling Commission shall attach the following documents to the report
                  for onward transmission to the Divisional Supervisory Commission:
                   - blank ballot papers;
                   - envelopes and ballot papers that were subject to controversy;
                   - counting sheets signed by each scrutineer;
                   - any other relevant document relating to the conduct of the poll.
(2) The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall enclose the documents
    specified in Subparagraph (1) above in an envelope addressed to: “The Chair-
    person of the Divisional Supervisory Commission”.
(3) He/she shall seal the said envelope and hand it to the ELECAM Council Branch
    Head, for onward transmission, not later than 48 (forty eight) hours after the clo-
    sing of the poll, to the Chairperson of the Divisional Supervisory Commission.
(4) The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall also forward the original of
    the report to the ELECAM Council Branch Head for filing. This original shall be
    considered authentic in case of any dispute.

                                                  CHAPTER V

                           MISCELLANEOUS AND FINAL PROVISIONS


ARTICLE 22.- Any disturbance of public order and measures taken to check them,
             as well as any other relevant comment, received or made by the Com-


  158      GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS
                                                                                                   ANNEX XII




                 mission on the conduct of the poll shall be mentioned in the Local Pol-
                 ling Commission’s report.

ARTICLE 23.- The marked electoral roll shall be signed by members of the Local Pol-
             ling Commission that performed the secretariat duties of the Commis-
             sion.

ARTICLE 24.- The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall, at the end of the
             abovementioned exercises, retrieve the materials used in the conduct
             of the poll and hand same over to the competent ELECAM Council
             Branch Head.

ARTICLE 25.- This decision shall take effect as from the date of signature and shall be
             registered and published wherever necessary. /-


                                                                                  Yaounde, 15 September 2011


                                                                     THE DIRECTOR GENERAL OF ELECTIONS

                                                                                                                  (ed)

                                                                                      MOHAMAN SANI TANIMOU




  GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS   159

                                                 ORDER No. 001/DIPL/CAB of 24 AUGUST 2011
                                                          to establish the list of Diplomatic
                                                          Representations and Consular Posts
                                                          where in election operations shall be
                                                          organized relating to the election of the!


                                     THE MINISTER OF EXTERNAL RELATIONS,


  Mindful            of the Constitution;
  Mindful            of Law No. 92/010 of 17 September 1997 to lay down conditions governing
                     the vacancy of and election to the Presidency of the Republic, amended
                     and supplemented by Law No. 97/020 of 9 September 1997 and by Law
                     No. 2011/002 of 6 May 2011;
  Mindful            of Law No. 2011/013 of 13 July 2011 relating to voting by
                     Cameroonians citizens settled or residing abroad
  Mindful            of Decree No. 2004/320 of 8 December 2004 to organize the Government,
                     amended and supplemented by Decree No. 2007/268 of 7 September
                     2007;
  Mindful            of Decree No. 2007/269 of 7 September 2007 to reshuffle the Government;
  Mindful            of Decree No. 2011/237 of 8 August 2011 to lay down conditions for
                     implementing Law No. 2011/013 of 13 July 2011 relating to voting by
                     Cameroonians settled or residing abroad,


                                           HEREBY ORDERS AS FOLLOWS:
  ARTICLE 1.- Election operations relating to the election of the President of the
  Republic in 2011 shall be organized within the Diplomatic Representations and
  Consular Posts of Cameroon classified, by zones and per country, in
  conformity with the list below:

                                              DECISION NO.0429/ELECAM/DGE OF 16 SEPTEMBER 2011
                                              to lay down the organisation of polling stations at diplo-
                                              matic representations and consular posts on the occasion
                                              of the October 2011 presidential election


                                       THE DIRECTOR GENERAL OF ELECTIONS,

 Mindful of          the Constitution;

 Mindful of          Law No. 92/010 of 17 September 1992 to lay down conditions governing the
                     vacancy of and election to the Presidency of the Republic and its subse-
                     quent amendments;

 Mindful of          Law No. 2006/11 of 29 December 2006 to set up and lay down the organisa-
                     tion and functioning of Elections Cameroon (ELECAM) and its subsequent
                     amendments;

 Mindful of          Law No. 2011/013 of 13 July 2011 relating to voting by Cameroonians settled
                     or residing abroad;

 Mindful of          Decree No. 2008/470 of 31 December 2006 appointing Officials at Elections
                     Cameroon;

 Mindful of          Decree No. 2011/237 of 8 August 2011 to lay down conditions for implemen-
                     ting Law No. 2011/013 of 13 July 2011 relating to voting by Cameroonians
                     settled or residing abroad;

 Mindful of          Decree No. 2011/277 of 30 August convening electors to the election of the
                     President of the Republic;

 Mindful of          Order No. 001/DIP/CAB of 24 August 2011 to establish the list of diplomatic
                     representations and consular posts wherein are organised election opera-
                     tions relating to the Election of the President of the Republic in 2011,

                                            HEREBY DECIDES AS FOLLOWS:

                                                          CHAPTER I

                                                  GENERAL PROVISIONS


 ARTICLE 1.- (1) This decision determines the organisation of polling Stations at diplomatic
                 representations and consular posts, on the occasion of the October 2011
                 presidential election.

     GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS        187
            ANNEX XVII




(2) It is hereby taken, in accordance with Section 22 (1) of the abovementioned Law
No. 2006/011 of 29 December 2006, to define, notably:

  - activities preceding polling operations;
  - polling procedure;
  - vote counting procedure.


                                         CHAPTER II
                         ACTIVITIES PRECEDING POLLING OPERATIONS


SECTION I : ELECTION MATERIALS

ARTICLE 2.- (1) Each polling station shall be located on premises arranged at the diplo-
                matic representations or consular posts, furnished with the following ma-
                terials:

  - one ballot box;
  - one polling booth;
  - seals;
  - one disposal bag;
  - one stamp pad with indelible ink;
  - one date stamp;
  - ball pens;
  - one calculator;
  - two (2) copies of the electoral roll;
  - one copy of the decision to lay down the organisation of polling stations;
  - one copy of the Guide to Chairpersons and Members of Local Polling Commis-
    sions;
  - ballot papers of each running candidate;
  - vote count return reports;
  - vote counting sheets;
  - appropriate envelopes;
  - office stationery.

(2) The materials listed in Subparagraph (1) above shall be placed on the table where
    members of the Local Polling Commission are sitting, with the exception of the pol-
    ling booth, the disposal bag and the ballot box.

(3) The ballot box, envelopes and ballot papers shall be kept under the permanent su-
    pervision of the Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission.

(4) The ballot box shall be placed in the middle of the hall, in front of the table where
    the polling commission members are seated.

ARTICLE 3.- (1) The polling booth shall be placed in the hall wherein the polling takes
                place and in full view of the electors. It shall be placed such as to shield
                the gestures of the elector from the view of the other electors, members
                of the Local Polling Commission and the general public.



  188       GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS
                                                                                                    ANNEX XVII




(2) One disposal bag shall be placed within each polling booth into which each elector
must throw the unused ballot papers.

                                      SECTION II : OPENING OF THE POLL

ARTICLE 4.- The poll must be open at 8:00 a.m. prompt.

ARTICLE 5.- (1) Before the first vote is cast, the Chairperson of the Local Polling Commis-
                sion shall open the ballot box to let all the members of the Commission,
                duly accredited observers and the electors present ascertain that it is
                empty and is provided with a single opening for the envelopes.

(2) The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall then close the ballot box again
    under the watchful eyes of the persons mentioned in Subparagraph (1) above and
    seal it.

                                                      CHAPTER III

                                               POLLING PROCEDURE


                          SECTION I : ACCOMPLISHING THE VOTING EXERCISE

ARTICLE 6.- (1) Before casting his/her vote, the elector shall be identified by the Chairper-
                son or a member of the Local Polling Commission who should ensure that
                his/her full names and voter’s card number are consistent with those ente-
                red in the electoral roll.

(2) Once the formality provided for in Subparagraph (1) is completed, the elector shall,
    under the supervision of the Chairperson and members of the Local Polling Commis-
    sion, personally take the ballot paper of each candidate and an envelope.

(3) With the election materials specified in Subparagraph (2) above, the elector shall
    enter the polling booth and select the ballot paper of his/her choice.

(4) He/she shall then come out of the polling booth and slip the envelope containing the
    chosen ballot paper into the ballot box.

ARTICLE 7.- The vote of the elector shall be evidenced by:

  - affixing the sign (X) before his/her name on the electoral roll, in the column provided
    for that purpose;
  - entering the date of the poll on the voter’s card in the space provided for that pur-
    pose;
  - affixing the elector’s thumb print, taken from the upper part of his/her thumb where
    the nail ends and marked with an indelible ink, on the voter’s card;
  - marking the elector’s thumbnail with indelible ink.

ARTICLE 8.- Any elector duly registered on the electoral roll, without a voter’s card, shall
            be allowed to vote, provided he/she is formally identified by the Commission.



   GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS   189
            ANNEX XVII




ARTICLE 9.- (1) Any elector who is unable to personally accomplish the voting exercise
                specified in Article 6 above, may enlist the assistance of an elector of
                his/her choice, registered in the electoral roll of the polling station concer-
                ned.

(2) The elector enlisted shall be neither a candidate nor the representative of a candi-
    date.

(3) He/she may not assist more than one person on the polling day.

(4) Voting by proxy shall not be allowed.

ARTICLE 10.- Besides, the chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall take all appro-
             priate measures in favour of persons with disabilities, pregnant women and el-
             derly voters.


ARTICLE 11.- After the vote is cast, the Local Polling Commission shall return the voter’s
             card to the elector; who shall immediately leave the hall. He/she shall not be
             allowed to talk to the Chairperson, members of the Local Polling Commission
             and observers.

                                    SECTION III :
             ROLE OF THE CHAIRPERSON OF THE LOCAL POLLING COMMISSION

ARTICLE 12.- (1) The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall ensure the presence
                 of, at least, two (2) members of the Local Polling Commission in the station
                 throughout the polling exercise.

(2) The Chairperson must strive to be present throughout the polling exercise.

ARTICLE 13.- (1) Only members of the Local Polling Commission shall be allowed to remain
                 in the hall where the polling exercise is taking place.

(2) Notwithstanding the provisions of Subparagraph (1) the officials of diplomatic represen-
tations and consular posts as well as international observers may remain in the polling sta-
tion concerned, provided that such presence does not disrupt the conduct of the poll.

ARTICLE 14.- In case an individual carrying a weapon or dangerous device enters the pol-
             ling station where voting is taking place, causes unlawful crowd gathering,
             threatens electors or attempts to forcefully take away the ballot box or any
             other election materials provided for in Article 2 (1) above, the Chairperson of
             the Local Polling Commission shall order the said individual to leave and, if
             necessary, the head of the diplomatic representation or consular post
             concerned shall, in keeping with his/her role of ensuring the general security
             of the poll, restore order in the station or cause the station to be evacuated,
             in collaboration with the competent authorities of the country of accredita-
             tion.

ARTICLE 15.- (1) At 6:00 p.m. prompt, the Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall
                 declare the poll closed.


  190       GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS
                                                                                                    ANNEX XVII




(2) However, electors already present inside the hall at the closing hour, as well as those
waiting outside the door to enter the hall, shall be allowed to cast their votes.
(3) After declaring the poll closed, the Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall
    seal the opening of the ballot box and take all necessary measures for the smooth
    conduct of the vote counting exercise.

(4) Once the poll is closed, the hall in which the polling exercise took place shall remain
    open until the vote counting is completed.


                                                       CHAPTER IV

                                           VOTE COUNTING PROCEDURE


ARTICLE 16.- (1) Prior to the vote counting exercise, the Chairperson of the Local Polling
                 Commission shall appoint two (2) scrutineers from among the electors en-
                 tered on the electoral rolls of the polling station concerned.

(2) The names of these scrutineers shall be then mentioned in the vote count return report
    by the member performing the secretariat duties of the Commission.

(3) The member of the Local Polling Commission referred to in Subparagraph (2) above
    shall also prepare the vote counting sheets.

ARTICLE 17.- (1) At the end of the polling exercise, any elector who wishes to attend the
                 vote counting shall be allowed to enter the hall in which the Local Polling
                 Commission is conducting the vote count, depending on the capacity of
                 the said hall.

(2) The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission may, in case of disturbance, expel
    any troublemaker and/or demand the intervention of the head of the diplomatic re-
    presentation or consular post concerned, who shall expel the said troublemaker, in
    conformity with the law in force in the country of accreditation.

ARTICLE 18.- Before opening the ballot box for the vote counting exercise, the Chairperson
             of the Local Polling Commission concerned shall place a table, in the middle
             of the hall, on which the appointed scrutineers shall pour out all the ballot pa-
             pers contained in the ballot box, for counting.

ARTICLE 19.- (1) The vote counting exercise shall be conducted in accordance with the
                 provisions of Sections 85 to 92 of Law No. 92/10 of 17 September 1992 to
                 lay down conditions governing the vacancy of and election to the Presi-
                 dency of the Republic, and its subsequent amendments.

(2) The following shall not be taken into:

  - ballot papers other than those printed officially;
  - ballot papers with any mark or sign;
  - ballot papers found in envelopes other than those put at the disposal of the electors;
  - ballot papers of the various candidates found in the same envelope.


   GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS   191
            ANNEX XVII




(3) The ballot papers thus rejected and, where appropriate, the envelopes containing
    them shall be signed by the members of the Local Polling Commission and attached
    to the report in which their number shall also be indicated.

(4) Ballot papers found in the ballot box without envelopes and empty envelopes shall
    be considered invalid and signed by the members of the competent Local Polling
    Commission. This shall be mentioned in the report.


ARTICLE 20.- (1) The results of the poll thus obtained in each polling station shall be im-
                 mediately announced on the spot and recorded in the report. The said
                 report shall be drawn up in as many copies as there are members, plus
                 two (2) other copies; closed and signed by the members of the Local
                 Polling Commission.

(2) The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall forward the original as well as
    two (2) copies of the report to the head of the diplomatic representation or consular
    post. The original shall be filed in the archives at the diplomatic representation or
    consular post. This original shall be considered authentic in case of any dispute.

(3) One copy of the report shall be immediately forwarded by the head of the diploma-
    tic representation or consular post to the Minister in charge of External Relations for
    onward transmission to the Chairperson of the National Commission for the Final
    Counting of Votes, through any means leaving a paper trail.

(4) The following shall be attached to the copy of the report mentioned in Subpara-
    graph (3) above:

  - blank ballot papers;
  - envelopes and ballot papers that were subject to controversy;
  - counting sheets signed by each scrutineer;
  - any other relevant document relating to the conduct of the poll.

(5) The other copy shall be forwarded to the Directorate General of Elections in com-
pliance with the same provisions set out in Subparagraph (3) above.

                                                  CHAPTER V

                             MISCELLANEOUS AND FINAL PROVISIONS


ARTICLE 21.- Any disturbance of public order and measures taken to check them, as
             well as any other relevant comment, received or made by the Commission
             on the conduct of the poll shall be mentioned in the Local Polling Commis-
             sion’s report.

ARTICLE 22.- The marked electoral roll shall be signed by the member who performed
             the secretariat duties of the Commission.




  192       GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS
                                                                                                    ANNEX XVII




ARTICLE 23.- The Chairperson of the Local Polling Commission shall, at the end of the abo-
             vementioned exercises, retrieve the materials used in the conduct of the poll
             and hand same over to the head of the diplomatic representation or consu-
             lar post for safekeeping.

ARTICLE 24.- This decision shall take effect as from the date of signature and shall be regis-
             tered and published wherever necessary. /-

                                                                                      Yaounde, 16 September 2011

                                                                          THE DIRECTOR GENERAL OF ELECTIONS

                                                                                                                    (ed)

                                                                                          MOHAMAN SANI TANIMOU




   GENERAL REPORT ON THE CONDUCT OF THE OCTOBER 9, 2011 PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION - DIRECTORATE GENERAL OF ELECTIONS   193
